keywords: dividend kings, consumer staples, valuation comparison, pet nutrition, acquisition integration, price-to-earnings ratio
headline: Colgate-Palmolive vs. Kimberly-Clark: Which Household Name Deserves Your Investment Dollars?
<<<SUMMARY>>>
Colgate-Palmolive and Kimberly-Clark are both Dividend Kings with strong track records, but they differ in valuation and growth strategy. Colgate-Palmolive trades at a premium (24.3x forward P/E) with growth driven by its Hill's Pet Nutrition segment, while Kimberly-Clark offers a lower valuation (14.7x forward P/E) but faces integration challenges from its Kenvue acquisition. The choice depends on whether investors believe in Colgate's pet food expansion or Kimberly-Clark's expanded hygiene portfolio.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
CL | positive | Strong global market leadership in oral care, successful Hill's Pet Nutrition segment generating $1.2B in Q3 2026 with 5.5% growth representing 22% of total sales, 64-year dividend increase streak, and diversified revenue streams position it as a reliable growth investment despite premium valuation.
KMB | neutral | Offers attractive valuation at 14.7x forward P/E and 54-year dividend increase streak, but faces headwinds including 2.1% revenue decline in FY 2025, volatile raw material costs, and uncertainty around Kenvue acquisition synergies. The expanded product portfolio is promising but execution risk remains.
WMT | neutral | Mentioned as a major customer for both Colgate-Palmolive (11% of net sales) and Kimberly-Clark (16% of net sales), representing significant customer concentration risk for both companies.
PG | neutral | Identified as a constant competitive rival to Colgate-Palmolive in core markets, representing ongoing competitive pressure.

Colgate offers global stability through oral care and pet nutrition, while Hershey benefits from resilient American snacking demand.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
CL | neutral | Solid fundamentals with $21B revenue, strong free cash flow of $3.8B, and global brand recognition. However, higher valuation (37.35 P/E), lower dividend yield (2.2%), and risks from geopolitical instability and talc litigation make it less attractive than Hershey for 2027.
HSY | positive | Recommended as the better buy for 2027 due to lower forward P/E (19.1x), higher dividend yield (3.4%), and attractive valuation following cocoa price pressures. Strong revenue growth (6.7%), solid free cash flow ($2.1B), and resilient snacking portfolio support long-term growth potential.
